By the time I go to work, I just feel like I never actually rested.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things?\n\nPatient: Not really about a lot of different things, no. I mean, I stress about work and the client portfolios, like I mentioned. But I wouldn't say I'm worrying about... I don't know, everything else in life. It's not a bunch of different topics. It's mostly just the work stuff, and otherwise I just feel completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Most days, whether I'm moving through the office or just back at my apartment, I have this heavy, gray feeling. It's like I'm just going through the motions... and honestly, I spend a lot of time just trying not to cry when someone asks me a completely normal question.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen at work before all of this started?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not like there was one specific incident recently. It's actually been going on for about three years now. It started right around the time the merger happened and they completely reorganized our Caribbean-market division. Ever since then, it's just been this constant, heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think I've lost interest in anything specifically. It's more just... I'm so exhausted all the time from that heavy feeling I mentioned, so I might not do as much, but it's not that I don't enjoy things anymore. I just don't have the energy for them.\n\nDoctor: If your work situation improved, do you think this heavy feeling would lift?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... it started with all that reorganization, sure, but it's been three years now. I'm not sure if things getting better at the office would actually make a difference anymore. It just feels like it's always there, regardless of what kind of day I'm having at work. So... I really couldn't say if it would lift.\n\nDoctor: For most of the past three years, have you felt this heavy, gray mood on more days than not?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely more days than not. Actually, it's most days. I just... I move through the office, and even my apartment, with this heavy, gray feeling. It really is like I'm just going through the motions. And honestly, half the time I'm just trying not to cry when someone asks me a completely normal question.\n\nDoctor: During these past three years, has there ever been a stretch of two months or longer when that heavy, gray feeling completely went away?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. I mean... maybe there's a day or two here and there where it doesn't feel quite as crushing, but... no, it's never completely gone away for a stretch of two months. It's pretty much just always there.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often felt badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I just... I feel like I'm not good enough anymore. Like at work, if a manager questions one of my financial projections, even just a normal question, I immediately take it as confirmation that I'm not as sharp as I used to be. It just feels like everyone can see that I'm failing.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, has your appetite often been poor?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my appetite has been pretty normal, I think. I haven't really noticed any changes with eating or anything... so, no, I wouldn't say it's been poor.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often had trouble concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. It's been really noticeable at work. Spreadsheets that used to be second nature to me... they now take me hours to get through. I just lose the thread of what I'm doing. I'll find myself rereading the exact same assumptions over and over again, and even after all that, I still worry I've missed something really obvious right before a presentation. It's just very hard to focus my mind.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often had low energy or felt fatigued?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my energy levels are mostly fine. I get tired at the end of a long day just like anyone else, but I haven't noticed feeling physically fatigued or having unusually low energy. It's really just the mental focus that's been the issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often felt hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say hopeless. I mean, I definitely get frustrated with myself, especially with the work stuff I mentioned, but... I don't feel like things are hopeless. I just feel like I'm not functioning as well as I should be. But I still have hope that things can get better, or at least back to normal.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I really couldn't say if it would lift.\n\nDoctor: For most of the past three years, have you felt this heavy, gray mood on more days than not?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely more days than not. Actually, it's most days. I just... I move through the office, and even my apartment, with this heavy, gray feeling. It really is like I'm just going through the motions. And honestly, half the time I'm just trying not to cry when someone asks me a completely normal question.\n\nDoctor: During these past three years, has there ever been a stretch of two months or longer when that heavy, gray feeling completely went away?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. I mean... maybe there's a day or two here and there where it doesn't feel quite as crushing, but... no, it's never completely gone away for a stretch of two months. It's pretty much just always there.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often felt badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I just... I feel like I'm not good enough anymore. Like at work, if a manager questions one of my financial projections, even just a normal question, I immediately take it as confirmation that I'm not as sharp as I used to be. It just feels like everyone can see that I'm failing.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, has your appetite often been poor?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my appetite has been pretty normal, I think. I haven't really noticed any changes with eating or anything... so, no, I wouldn't say it's been poor.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often had trouble concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. It's been really noticeable at work. Spreadsheets that used to be second nature to me... they now take me hours to get through. I just lose the thread of what I'm doing. I'll find myself rereading the exact same assumptions over and over again, and even after all that, I still worry I've missed something really obvious right before a presentation. It's just very hard to focus my mind.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often had low energy or felt fatigued?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my energy levels are mostly fine. I get tired at the end of a long day just like anyone else, but I haven't noticed feeling physically fatigued or having unusually low energy. It's really just the mental focus that's been the issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often felt hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say hopeless. I mean, I definitely get frustrated with myself, especially with the work stuff I mentioned, but... I don't feel like things are hopeless. I just feel like I'm not functioning as well as I should be. But I still have hope that things can get better, or at least back to normal.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have there been periods when these symptoms were present nearly all day, every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure I'd describe it quite like that. It hasn't been an all day, every day thing for two weeks straight. It's definitely a frequent problem, especially when I'm at my desk trying to run numbers, but... no, I don't think it's been a constant, every single minute kind of thing like you're describing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. And then I end up waking up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach completely tight. By the time I go to work, I just feel like I never actually rested.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things?\n\nPatient: Not really about a lot of different things, no. I mean, I stress about work and the client portfolios, like I mentioned. But I wouldn't say I'm worrying about... I don't know, everything else in life. It's not a bunch of different topics. It's mostly just the work stuff, and otherwise I just feel completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Most days, whether I'm moving through the office or just back at my apartment, I have this heavy, gray feeling. It's like I'm just going through the motions... and honestly, I spend a lot of time just trying not to cry when someone asks me a completely normal question.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen at work before all of this started?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not like there was one specific incident recently. It's actually been going on for about three years now. It started right around the time the merger happened and they completely reorganized our Caribbean-market division. Ever since then, it's just been this constant, heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think I've lost interest in anything specifically. It's more just... I'm so exhausted all the time from that heavy feeling I mentioned, so I might not do as much, but it's not that I don't enjoy things anymore. I just don't have the energy for them.\n\nDoctor: If your work situation improved, do you think this heavy feeling would lift?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... it started with all that reorganization, sure, but it's been three years now. I'm not sure if things getting better at the office would actually make a difference anymore. It just feels like it's always there, regardless of what kind of day I'm having at work. So... I really couldn't say if it would lift.\n\nDoctor: For most of the past three years, have you felt this heavy, gray mood on more days than not?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely more days than not. Actually, it's most days. I just... I move through the office, and even my apartment, with this heavy, gray feeling. It really is like I'm just going through the motions. And honestly, half the time I'm just trying not to cry when someone asks me a completely normal question.\n\nDoctor: During these past three years, has there ever been a stretch of two months or longer when that heavy, gray feeling completely went away?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. I mean... maybe there's a day or two here and there where it doesn't feel quite as crushing, but... no, it's never completely gone away for a stretch of two months. It's pretty much just always there.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often felt badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I just... I feel like I'm not good enough anymore. Like at work, if a manager questions one of my financial projections, even just a normal question, I immediately take it as confirmation that I'm not as sharp as I used to be. It just feels like everyone can see that I'm failing.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, has your appetite often been poor?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my appetite has been pretty normal, I think. I haven't really noticed any changes with eating or anything... so, no, I wouldn't say it's been poor.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often had trouble concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. It's been really noticeable at work. Spreadsheets that used to be second nature to me... they now take me hours to get through. I just lose the thread of what I'm doing. I'll find myself rereading the exact same assumptions over and over again, and even after all that, I still worry I've missed something really obvious right before a presentation. It's just very hard to focus my mind.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often had low energy or felt fatigued?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my energy levels are mostly fine. I get tired at the end of a long day just like anyone else, but I haven't noticed feeling physically fatigued or having unusually low energy. It's really just the mental focus that's been the issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often felt hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say hopeless. I mean, I definitely get frustrated with myself, especially with the work stuff I mentioned, but... I don't feel like things are hopeless. I just feel like I'm not functioning as well as I should be. It hasn't been an all day, every day thing for two weeks straight. I mean, I don't spend my day worrying about sleep. I just focus on trying to get my work done. Sleep isn't really something I stress about during the day.\n\nDoctor: When you have trouble sleeping, are you giving yourself enough time in bed to sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF34.1, F32.9, F43.2, F51.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because I barely sleep in any useful way anymore. I just... I'll lie awake at night replaying client models in my head. And then I end up waking up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach completely tight. By the time I go to work, I just feel like I never actually rested.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things?\n\nPatient: Not really about a lot of different things, no. I mean, I stress about work and the client portfolios, like I mentioned. But I wouldn't say I'm worrying about... I don't know, everything else in life. It's not a bunch of different topics. It's mostly just the work stuff, and otherwise I just feel completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Most days, whether I'm moving through the office or just back at my apartment, I have this heavy, gray feeling. It's like I'm just going through the motions... and honestly, I spend a lot of time just trying not to cry when someone asks me a completely normal question.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen at work before all of this started?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not like there was one specific incident recently. It's actually been going on for about three years now. It started right around the time the merger happened and they completely reorganized our Caribbean-market division. Ever since then, it's just been this constant, heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think I've lost interest in anything specifically. It's more just... I'm so exhausted all the time from that heavy feeling I mentioned, so I might not do as much, but it's not that I don't enjoy things anymore. I just don't have the energy for them.\n\nDoctor: If your work situation improved, do you think this heavy feeling would lift?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... it started with all that reorganization, sure, but it's been three years now. I'm not sure if things getting better at the office would actually make a difference anymore. It just feels like it's always there, regardless of what kind of day I'm having at work. So... I really couldn't say if it would lift.\n\nDoctor: For most of the past three years, have you felt this heavy, gray mood on more days than not?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely more days than not. Actually, it's most days. I just... I move through the office, and even my apartment, with this heavy, gray feeling. It really is like I'm just going through the motions. And honestly, half the time I'm just trying not to cry when someone asks me a completely normal question.\n\nDoctor: During these past three years, has there ever been a stretch of two months or longer when that heavy, gray feeling completely went away?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. I mean... maybe there's a day or two here and there where it doesn't feel quite as crushing, but... no, it's never completely gone away for a stretch of two months. It's pretty much just always there.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often felt badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I just... I feel like I'm not good enough anymore. Like at work, if a manager questions one of my financial projections, even just a normal question, I immediately take it as confirmation that I'm not as sharp as I used to be. It just feels like everyone can see that I'm failing.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, has your appetite often been poor?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my appetite has been pretty normal, I think. I haven't really noticed any changes with eating or anything... so, no, I wouldn't say it's been poor.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often had trouble concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. It's been really noticeable at work. Spreadsheets that used to be second nature to me... they now take me hours to get through. I just lose the thread of what I'm doing. I'll find myself rereading the exact same assumptions over and over again, and even after all that, I still worry I've missed something really obvious right before a presentation. It's just very hard to focus my mind.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often had low energy or felt fatigued?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my energy levels are mostly fine. I get tired at the end of a long day just like anyone else, but I haven't noticed feeling physically fatigued or having unusually low energy. It's really just the mental focus that's been the issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often felt hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say hopeless. I mean, I definitely get frustrated with myself, especially with the work stuff I mentioned, but... I don't feel like things are hopeless. I just feel like I'm not functioning as well as I should be. But I still have hope that things can get better, or at least back to normal.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have there been periods when these symptoms were present nearly all day, every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure I'd describe it quite like that. It hasn't been an all day, every day thing for two weeks straight. It's definitely a frequent problem, especially when I'm at my desk trying to run numbers, but... no, I don't think it's been a constant, every single minute kind of thing like you're describing.\n\nDoctor: During the day, do you spend a lot of time worrying about not being able to sleep?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't spend my day worrying about sleep. I just focus on trying to get my work done. Sleep isn't really something I stress about during the day.\n\nDoctor: When you have trouble sleeping, are you giving yourself enough time in bed to sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I make sure to go to bed at a reasonable hour, usually around 10 or 10:30. So the time is definitely there... I'm in bed for a solid eight hours before I have to get up for work. It's just the actual sleeping part that doesn't always happen, not the amount of time I set aside for it.\n\nDoctor: When you're away from work for several days, does that heavy, gray feeling still stay with you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. It doesn't really matter if I'm at work or at home in my apartment... most days, I just have this heavy, gray feeling hanging over me. It's like I'm just going through the motions of my life. Honestly, it's at the point where I'm just trying not to cry when someone asks me a normal, everyday question. So, yes, it stays with me even when I'm away from the office.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I mean, I stress about work and the client portfolios, like I mentioned. But I wouldn't say I'm worrying about... I don't know, everything else in life. It's not a bunch of different topics. It's mostly just the work stuff, and otherwise I just feel completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Most days, whether I'm moving through the office or just back at my apartment, I have this heavy, gray feeling. It's like I'm just going through the motions... and honestly, I spend a lot of time just trying not to cry when someone asks me a completely normal question.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen at work before all of this started?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not like there was one specific incident recently. It's actually been going on for about three years now. It started right around the time the merger happened and they completely reorganized our Caribbean-market division. Ever since then, it's just been this constant, heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think I've lost interest in anything specifically. It's more just... I'm so exhausted all the time from that heavy feeling I mentioned, so I might not do as much, but it's not that I don't enjoy things anymore. I just don't have the energy for them.\n\nDoctor: If your work situation improved, do you think this heavy feeling would lift?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... it started with all that reorganization, sure, but it's been three years now. I'm not sure if things getting better at the office would actually make a difference anymore. It just feels like it's always there, regardless of what kind of day I'm having at work. So... I really couldn't say if it would lift.\n\nDoctor: For most of the past three years, have you felt this heavy, gray mood on more days than not?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely more days than not. Actually, it's most days. I just... I move through the office, and even my apartment, with this heavy, gray feeling. It really is like I'm just going through the motions. And honestly, half the time I'm just trying not to cry when someone asks me a completely normal question.\n\nDoctor: During these past three years, has there ever been a stretch of two months or longer when that heavy, gray feeling completely went away?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. I mean... maybe there's a day or two here and there where it doesn't feel quite as crushing, but... no, it's never completely gone away for a stretch of two months. It's pretty much just always there.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often felt badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I just... I feel like I'm not good enough anymore. Like at work, if a manager questions one of my financial projections, even just a normal question, I immediately take it as confirmation that I'm not as sharp as I used to be. It just feels like everyone can see that I'm failing.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, has your appetite often been poor?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my appetite has been pretty normal, I think. I haven't really noticed any changes with eating or anything... so, no, I wouldn't say it's been poor.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often had trouble concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. It's been really noticeable at work. Spreadsheets that used to be second nature to me... they now take me hours to get through. I just lose the thread of what I'm doing. I'll find myself rereading the exact same assumptions over and over again, and even after all that, I still worry I've missed something really obvious right before a presentation. It's just very hard to focus my mind.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often had low energy or felt fatigued?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my energy levels are mostly fine. I get tired at the end of a long day just like anyone else, but I haven't noticed feeling physically fatigued or having unusually low energy. It's really just the mental focus that's been the issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few years, have you often felt hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say hopeless. I mean, I definitely get frustrated with myself, especially with the work stuff I mentioned, but... I don't feel like things are hopeless. I just feel like I'm not functioning as well as I should be. But I still have hope that things can get better, or at least back to normal.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have there been periods when these symptoms were present nearly all day, every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure I'd describe it quite like that. It hasn't been an all day, every day thing for two weeks straight. It's definitely a frequent problem, especially when I'm at my desk trying to run numbers, but... no, I don't think it's been a constant, every single minute kind of thing like you're describing.\n\nDoctor: During the day, do you spend a lot of time worrying about not being able to sleep?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't spend my day worrying about sleep. I just focus on trying to get my work done. Sleep isn't really something I stress about during the day.\n\nDoctor: When you have trouble sleeping, are you giving yourself enough time in bed to sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I make sure to go to bed at a reasonable hour, usually around 10 or 10:30. So the time is definitely there... I'm in bed for a solid eight hours before I have to get up for work. It's just the actual sleeping part that doesn't always happen, not the amount of time I set aside for it.\n\nDoctor: When you're away from work for several days, does that heavy, gray feeling still stay with you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. It doesn't really matter if I'm at work or at home in my apartment... most days, I just have this heavy, gray feeling hanging over me. It's like I'm just going through the motions of my life. Honestly, it's at the point where I'm just trying not to cry when someone asks me a normal, everyday question. So, yes, it stays with me even when I'm away from the office.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms made it hard for you to function well at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, it really has. At work, um... I actually got a formal performance warning recently. My financial models and presentations just keep slipping, and I can't seem to keep up with them anymore. \n\nAnd outside of work, it's not any better. I've completely stopped showing up for the things that used to keep my life intact. I used to do volunteer teaching, go to yoga, go to my book club... but I just don't go to any of it now. I just don't have it in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or euphoric for several days?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I've never felt anything like that. Honestly, it's just been this same low, heavy feeling for... well, for a long time now. I can't even remember the last time I felt just normal, let alone unusually energetic or euphoric.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms caused problems with your work or with keeping up your usual activities?\n\nPatient: I think I mentioned this a moment ago, but yes, it really has. Like I was saying, it's affecting my job enough that I got a formal performance warning recently. My financial models and presentations are just slipping, and I can't keep up. \n\nAnd as for my usual activities... yeah, I've completely stopped showing up for my volunteer teaching, yoga, and my book club. All those things that used to keep my life outside of work intact, I just don't do them anymore."
